## 9. Wave 10: upgrade deltas and visible form proof

**Implementer:** Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-13, DX2 commission.
Status of this amendment: Review until both commissioned blind reviews are READY.

A check remembers its compiler version, schema source identity, emitted API,
note counts by code and refusal counts in `.snapback4/check-surface.json`.
The first check using a different version compares that baseline with the same
project: newly added notes by code, changed generated row/operation types, and
new compiler refusals. If the source changed too, the delta says so; it does not
attribute application edits to the compiler. Missing baseline means explicitly
unavailable comparison. Compiler refusals preserve the last baseline and generated
API, so a refusal cannot erase the useful pre-upgrade record. Normal same-version
checks refresh it after successful check. A server/native qualification failure
can leave regenerated, valid Quarry types while retaining the previous baseline. JSON check output stays one
JSON object; human delta goes to stderr in that mode. Snapshot write failures
are reported and never invalidate valid product types. No store adoption, sync
or authority change is introduced.

Upgrade takes the old installed CLI's check snapshot before installation, then
invokes the newly installed CLI's check after pinning. It preserves the package
manager's dependency sections and ordinary recovery. A compiler refusal after a
successful install returns nonzero with a message distinguishing installation
from qualification; it never rolls back the packages silently. Module-only backends are projects too: all `snapback/*.q` files qualify through
the same loader; schema.q is not required. Without any Quarry source, upgrade
reports package pins only. Exact apps receive one rebuild pointer:
`bun exact.mjs snapback --rebuild` (HS owns the command and device handshake).
`guide changes` packages the release card; its version is bound to the compiler
version and its factual nullable-count examples are covered by the guide tests.
Nullable numeric aggregate writes offer `EXPR ?? 0` as an authored choice, not
auto-apply: hidden keys remain null, authorized missing count groups return zero.

The witness uses the declared persona independently of a guest-only sign-in
screen by selecting createApp's normal persona option under the explicit
witness flag. It still verifies the actual viewer before writing. Custom apps
retain their visible persona picker; a missing persona app/picker refuses. No persona
works against serve. Default starter examples use guest sign-in; `--as alice`
on a starter that declares alice also works.

A visible form proof is opt-in with `--field LABEL --value TEXT --button LABEL`.
It fills the labelled control, presses the labelled button and observes that
click's matching durable mutation through the registered app client. After filling it arms the exact labelled button, and accepts a mutation started
in that button's active Chromium click event (through either client or app).
The handler must submit the write before awaiting other work; perform asynchronous
validation before enabling submit. This scoped proof never attributes background
or input autosave writes to a button. Before clicking it arms a fresh observer: an existing sent status or row cannot satisfy
this proof. It waits for terminal sent and asserts the configured args when
provided; failed or timed-out custody is nonzero. No second SDK mutation is
injected. The ordinary SDK-only witness remains available without these flags.
React Native web uses keyboard events (`pressSequentially`) to settle state;
buttons use their accessible labels, not testID. Registration is flag-gated and
cleanup restores its instrumentation. Starters label their field, render write
status above the list, and supply a favicon.

Port selection precedence is explicit --port/--web-port, explicit --port-range,
then the complete PORT_LO/PORT_HI environment pair, then existing defaults.
Ranges are validated before writing any files, and every selected port lies in
them. The client URL, backend npm launcher, frontend dev/preview launcher,
README and witness script share those selections. Later runtime URL overrides
remain supported; the init range is a scaffold input, not an authority rule.
HS owns the Vite Node declarations; DX2 adds no parallel type shim.

Expo's web client and README derive their device-versus-journal choice from the
same compiled sync detection. Its smoke helper uses the schema's password,
guest or persona sign-in (password only on prime, with a witness-supplied test
credential; it never puts a secret in source). The helper is inert in ordinary
launches and other segments and waits for restoration. The native device and
normal identity custody remain unchanged.

Ordinary durable React `run()` owns an onWrite observer before admission and
waits for sent/failed, independently of read subscribers and mounted effects.
Early settlement cannot be missed and concurrent calls match only their own
saved/minted intent id. The promise releases its observer on terminal response
or thrown admission. Offline it waits while the client lives; closing the client
ends its ability to collect a receipt, so callers keep the App alive until
settlement or use the explicit custody option and saved id. Existing
`{ waitSeconds: 0 }` returns pending custody immediately; no pending result is
relabelled sent. This amends the default hook boundary, not client.mutate,
outbox durability, authority or replay-result availability. `sentResult` and
`otherwiseAll` already exist; the recipe and tests cover null, replay and all
five read meanings. No client card word is added.

Wire id omission already landed in §8 and is re-tested with the documented
full envelopes. Guides add tested loop writes and projection identity, list
supported formats (handle and url; email remains application validation), spell
out fixed-period UTC epoch anchoring and periodEnd, and carry the asset upload
route. Aliases resolve optional param, mutation default and service seat.
Non-TTY reads print a complete card; explicit section/find still narrows it.
REL2 owns release lifecycle narration, DIAG owns general diagnostics and PAGE2
owns planner/rules language; DX2's nullable-write guidance changes no semantics.


Wave-10 review record: round 1 Muse READY; fresh Sol NOT READY with four
material counterexamples. All accepted: preserve complete wave-9 starter byte
variants in adapt, recognize module-only projects, bind form admission to the
actual labelled button's active click, and refuse frontend overflow before
writing. Add controls for unrelated background writes and highest-port ranges.
The no-sync Todos README and stale piped-output wording are corrected as well.
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Time in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` is not a rule, because a rule that varies with time is not an audience a partition can hold. A row's life ends by the table line `expire at .col` instead: absent to every reader on every host from that time, swept by the server, the deletion streamed (see Moves).
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).

LLP 3000.004); 2026-09-13 (§7: one interpreter hosted two ways, the wasm measurement, ordered merge heads and measured performance work); 2026-09-12 (§8: the full-brief Clique record, 0.1.1–0.1.9); 2026-09-12 (§7: expiry as a lifetime, one asset one home, jobs as sweeps and schedules as boundaries; §8: 0.1.0, the whole brief); 2026-09-12 (§8: the local-first tier, H0); 2026-09-12 (§7: Quarry in Rust, one key encoding, sync forms, operation names; §8: the week-two day-one map); 2026-09-12 (§8: the week-one implementation map);  2026-09-12 (§7: toolchain, simulator host, the clock's commit); 2026-09-12 (initial: the author's decisions of 2026-09-12, the bet on one page, the bakeoff protocol, the fence, the first four weeks)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (Snapback 3, the sibling candidate), [LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) (the design brief this corpus builds), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md) (Snapback 2, the incumbent and the control), LLP 0000, LLP 0329, LLP 0329.014.004, LLP 0329.016, LLP 1001, LLP 1002, LLP 1012, LLP 1016, LLP 1019, LLP 1020, [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.004](./3000.004-feeds-and-the-interview-reference-app.rfc.md) (feeds; Interview as the second reference application), [LLP 3000.005](./3000.005-native-jobs.rfc.md) (native jobs), [LLP 3000.006](./3000.006-explicit-data-residency-and-server-execution.rfc.md) (explicit online-only data, execution classification and synced projections), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) (relaxed Quarry and explicit residency: the language's constraints re-derived under the meter), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md), [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md), [`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md)

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

codex
The diff adds the requested click ownership check at both client and App boundaries, and broadens project discovery to any top-level Quarry module. I’m now checking event lifetime, previous-template recognition, and README adaptation in both directions.

     4	
     5	type Operations = Record<string, unknown>;
     6	export function registerWebWitness(app: App, api: Operations): void {
     7	  if (typeof window === "undefined" || new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get("witness") !== "web") return;
     8	  const operations: unknown[] = [];
     9	  const walk = (value: Operations) => { for (const entry of Object.values(value)) {

    12	      else if (!("kind" in entry)) walk(entry as Operations);
    13	    }
    14	  } };
    15	  walk(api);
    16	  let form: { terminal: Promise<unknown>; cancel: () => void } | undefined;
    17	  Object.defineProperty(window, "__snapback4Witness", { configurable: true, value: {
    18	    ready: () => app.ready,
    19	    viewer: () => app.client?.viewer(),
    20	    beginForm: (name: string | undefined, expected: Record<string, unknown> | undefined, button: Element) => {
    21	      if (!app.client) throw Error("E_WITNESS_AUTH: no signed-in client");

    27	      // Chromium's active event ties admission to this actual control's handler.
    28	      // Timers, input autosaves and previously-started writes cannot claim it.
    29	      const capture = (event: Event) => {

    31	        // A submit button's default action dispatches a separate submit event.
    32	        else if (click && event.type === "submit" && (event as SubmitEvent).submitter === button) click = event;
    33	      };
    34	      document.addEventListener("click", capture, true);
    35	      document.addEventListener("submit", capture, true);
    36	      const restore = () => {
    37	        if (client.mutate === clientWrapper) client.mutate = originalClient;
    38	        if (app.mutate === appWrapper) app.mutate = originalApp;
    39	        document.removeEventListener("click", capture, true);
    40	        document.removeEventListener("submit", capture, true);
    41	      };
    42	      const canonical = (value: unknown): unknown => Array.isArray(value) ? value.map(canonical) : value && typeof value === "object"
    43	        ? Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(value).sort().map(key => [key, canonical((value as Record<string, unknown>)[key])])) : value;
    44	      const execute = async (original: Function, receiver: unknown, op: Mutation<any, any>, args: Record<string, unknown>, options?: any): Promise<Write> => {
    45	        const invoke = () => original.call(receiver, op, args, options) as Promise<Write>;
    46	        if (claimed || !click || window.event !== click || !operations.some(value => (value as Mutation<any, any>).name === op.name) || (name && name !== op.name)) return invoke();
    47	        claimed = true; restore();
    48	        if (expected && JSON.stringify(canonical(expected)) !== JSON.stringify(canonical(args))) {
    49	          resolve({ state: "failed", why: { code: "E_WITNESS_ARGUMENT" } });
    50	          return invoke();

    54	          if (id === undefined) { early.push(write); if (early.length > 256) early.shift(); }
    55	          else if (write.id === id) { stop(); resolve({ ...write, mutation: op.name }); }

     1	//! Starter leftovers on the init-first path: `init --web` (or `--expo`) wrote
     2	//! the todo starter, then the project replaced `snapback/schema.q`. The
     3	//! starter's own files that still match the bytes init wrote, but no longer
     4	//! fit the schema, are leftovers: `check` names them and `init --adapt`
     5	//! removes or rewrites them. A file the project changed is never touched.
     6	// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.2 — leftovers are judged by the starter's original bytes, never by name alone.
     7	use super::starter::{self, Body, Platform};
     8	use super::{OwnSchema, own_schema};
     9	use std::path::Path;
    10	
    11	/// One unmodified starter file that no longer fits the project's schema.
    12	pub(crate) struct Leftover {
    13	    pub path: &'static str,
    14	    pub why: String,
    15	    /// The text `init --adapt` writes; `None` removes the file.
    16	    pub rewrite: Option<String>,
    17	}
    18	
    19	/// Starter operations its fixtures, cases and `Todos` screen call.
    20	const STARTER_OPERATIONS: [&str; 4] = ["list", "add", "toggle", "remove"];
    21	
    22	/// Whether a schema still has the starter's `todos` table and the operations
    23	/// its fixtures, cases and `Todos` screen call.
    24	pub(super) fn fits_todos(own: &OwnSchema) -> bool {
    25	    own.tables.iter().any(|table| table == "todos")
    26	        && STARTER_OPERATIONS.iter().all(|op| own.operations.iter().any(|name| name == op))
    27	}
    28	
    29	/// What the schema in `dir` leaves of the starter, and the advice that goes
    30	/// with it (a sign-in the project must write by hand). No schema: nothing.
    31	pub(crate) fn leftovers(dir: &Path) -> (Vec<Leftover>, Vec<String>) {
    32	    let Ok(Some(own)) = own_schema(dir) else { return (Vec::new(), Vec::new()) };
    33	    let mut found = Vec::new();
    34	    let mut advice = Vec::new();
    35	    let todos = fits_todos(&own);
    36	    if !todos {
    37	        for (path, text) in starter::FIXTURES {
    38	            if read(dir, path).is_some_and(|current| current == super::without_design_references(text)) {
    39	                let what = if path.ends_with("todos.jsonl") { "rows for the todo starter's `todos` table" } else { "cases for the todo starter's list/add/toggle/remove" };
    40	                found.push(Leftover { path, why: format!("the unmodified {what}; your snapback/*.q has no todos table with those operations, so `snapback4 test` would refuse it"), rewrite: None });
    41	            }
    42	        }
    43	    }
    44	    for platform in [Platform::Web, Platform::Expo] {
    45	        screens(dir, platform, &own, todos, &mut found, &mut advice);
    46	    }
    47	    if let Some(text) = read(dir, "src/native-smoke.ts") {
    48	        let wanted = starter::native_smoke(own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests));
    49	        if text != wanted && [false, true].into_iter().any(|password| text == starter::native_smoke(password)) {
    50	            found.push(Leftover { path: "src/native-smoke.ts", why: "the unmodified native smoke helper uses the previous schema's sign-in".into(), rewrite: Some(wanted) });
    51	        }
    52	    }
    53	    readme(dir, &own, todos, &mut found);
    54	    app_json(dir, &mut found);
    55	    (found, advice)
    56	}
    57	
    58	/// The starter README, while it is still one init wrote (for either screen,
    59	/// at the ports it names), when it describes another screen than the schema
    60	/// now gets: the Todos README beside a project's schema, or the reverse.
    61	// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (042): adapt rewrote the screen but left a README about todos and guest sign-in.
    62	fn readme(dir: &Path, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
    63	    let platform = if dir.join("src/App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Web } else if dir.join("App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Expo } else { return };
    64	    let title = super::title(dir);
    65	    let personas = own.identity.is_none() && !own.personas.is_empty();
    66	    let wanted_body = if todos { Body::Todos } else { Body::Home(None) };
    67	    for path in ["README.md", "README.snapback.md"] {
    68	        let Some(text) = read(dir, path) else { continue };
    69	        let port = number_after(&text, "127.0.0.1:");
    70	        let web_port = number_after(&text, "localhost:");
    71	        let ports = [(port, web_port), (None, web_port), (port, None), (None, None)];
    72	        // The offline paragraph names the schema's synced tables when init
    73	        // wrote it; any of them (or 0.4.22's unconditional claim) still matches.
    74	        let masked = starter::without_offline(&text);
    75	        let matched = ports.into_iter().find(|&(port, web_port)| {
    76	            [(Body::Todos, false), (Body::Home(None), false), (Body::Home(None), true)].iter()
    77	                .any(|(body, personas)| [false, true].iter().any(|password| masked == starter::without_offline(&super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, body, *personas, &starter::Offline::Unknown, *password)), port, web_port))))
    78	        });
    79	        let Some((port, web_port)) = matched else { continue };
    80	        let wanted = super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, &wanted_body, personas, &own.offline, own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests))), port, web_port);
    81	        if wanted == text { continue; }
    82	        let about = if todos { "the todo starter's operations, which your snapback/*.q declares again" } else { "your snapback/*.q and the Home screen, named after this directory" };
    83	        let only_offline = !text.starts_with("# Todos") && starter::without_offline(&wanted) == masked;
    84	        // A schema that does not compile right now says nothing about what it syncs.
    85	        if only_offline && own.offline == starter::Offline::Unknown { continue; }
    86	        let why = if only_offline {
    87	            "the unmodified starter README says what works offline for another schema; adapt rewrites that paragraph from the tables your snapback/*.q syncs".to_owned()
    88	        } else {
    89	            format!("the unmodified starter README describes {}; adapt rewrites it to describe {about}", if text.starts_with("# Todos") { "the todo starter (its todos table and guest sign-in)" } else { "the Home screen for another schema" })
    90	        };
    91	        found.push(Leftover { path, why, rewrite: Some(wanted) });
    92	    }
    93	}
    94	
    95	/// An Expo `app.json` that still carries the todo starter's slug, scheme and
    96	/// bundle ids (written by 0.4.21 and earlier): adapt names them after the
    97	/// directory and changes nothing else in the file.
    98	// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (067): adapted Expo apps kept dev.snapback4.todos.
    99	fn app_json(dir: &Path, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
   100	    let Some(text) = read(dir, "app.json") else { return };
   101	    let Ok(config) = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&text) else { return };
   102	    let expo = &config["expo"];
   103	    let [(_, slug), (_, scheme), (_, bundle)] = starter::TODO_IDS;
   104	    if expo["slug"] != slug || expo["scheme"] != scheme || expo["ios"]["bundleIdentifier"] != bundle || expo["android"]["package"] != bundle { return; }
   105	    let (new_slug, new_scheme, new_bundle) = starter::app_ids(&super::title(dir));
   106	    let quoted = |value: &str| format!("\"{value}\"");
   107	    let rewrite = text.replace(&quoted(bundle), &quoted(&new_bundle)).replace(&quoted(slug), &quoted(&new_slug)).replace(&quoted(scheme), &quoted(&new_scheme));
   108	    found.push(Leftover { path: "app.json", why: format!("the todo starter's ids (slug {slug}, scheme {scheme}, bundle id {bundle}); adapt names them after this directory ({new_slug}, {new_scheme}, {new_bundle})"), rewrite: Some(rewrite) });
   109	}
   110	
   111	fn number_after(text: &str, prefix: &str) -> Option<u16> {
   112	    let at = text.find(prefix)? + prefix.len();
   113	    let digits: String = text[at..].chars().take_while(char::is_ascii_digit).collect();
   114	    digits.parse().ok()
   115	}
   116	
   117	/// The sign-in a schema admits, as the starter screen it needs: the guest
   118	/// screen (`None`), the persona picker (`Some(personas)`), or no starter
   119	/// screen fits (`Err(advice)`).
   120	#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
   121	enum SignIn { Guest, Password, Personas(Vec<String>) }
   122	impl SignIn {
   123	    fn label(&self) -> String {
   124	        match self { Self::Guest => "the guest sign-in".into(), Self::Password => "the password sign-in".into(), Self::Personas(names) => format!("a persona picker ({})", names.join(", ")) }
   125	    }
   126	    fn personas(&self) -> Option<&[String]> { match self { Self::Personas(names) => Some(names), _ => None } }
   127	}
   128	fn wanted(own: &OwnSchema) -> Result<SignIn, &'static str> {
   129	    match own.identity {
   130	        Some((_, true)) => Ok(SignIn::Guest),
   131	        Some((true, false)) => Ok(SignIn::Password),
   132	        None if !own.personas.is_empty() => Ok(SignIn::Personas(own.personas.clone())),
   133	        _ => Err("declares neither `use identity` nor `use personas`, so nobody can act: add `use identity` for the guest sign-in or `use personas alice, bob` for development, then rerun `snapback4 init --adapt`"),
   134	    }
   135	}
   136	
   137	fn screens(dir: &Path, platform: Platform, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>, advice: &mut Vec<String>) {
   138	    let (app_path, client_path) = platform.paths();
   139	    let (Some(app), Some(client)) = (read(dir, app_path), read(dir, client_path)) else { return };
   140	    // Which starter screen is on disk, if both files are still as init wrote
   141	    // them: its sign-in (guest, or a persona picker) and its body.
   142	    let journal_shape = starter::Offline::Tables { synced: vec![], total: 0 };
   143	    let client_shape = normalized(&client);
   144	    let matches_client = |reference: &str, personas: bool| [reference, starter::previous_client(platform, personas)].iter().any(|reference|
   145	        client_shape == normalized(reference) || client_shape == normalized(&starter::web_client((*reference).to_owned(), &journal_shape)));
   146	    let current = [Body::Todos, Body::Home(first_read(&app))].into_iter().find_map(|body| [false, true].into_iter().find_map(|previous| {
   147	        let screens = |personas: Option<&[String]>, password| if previous { starter::previous_screens(platform, personas, &body, password) } else { starter::identity_screens(platform, personas, &body, password) };
   148	        let (guest_app, guest_client) = screens(None, false);
   149	        let (persona_app, persona_client) = screens(Some(&[]), false);
   150	        let (password_app, password_client) = screens(None, true);
   151	        if app == guest_app && matches_client(&guest_client, false) { Some((SignIn::Guest, body.clone())) }
   152	        else if app == password_app && matches_client(&password_client, false) { Some((SignIn::Password, body.clone())) }
   153	        else if app == persona_app && matches_client(&persona_client, true) { Some((SignIn::Personas(personas_of(&client)), body.clone())) }
   154	        else { None }
   155	    }));
   156	    let Some((sign_in, body)) = current else { return };
   157	    // A Home keeps its read while the schema still declares that query
   158	    // argument-free; a Home without one stays as written (it fits any schema).
   159	    let wanted_body = match &body {
   160	        _ if todos => Body::Todos,
   161	        Body::Home(None) => Body::Home(None),
   162	        Body::Home(Some(op)) if own.reads.contains(op) => body.clone(),
   163	        _ => Body::Home(own.reads.first().cloned()),
   164	    };
   165	    // A sign-in no starter screen fits keeps the one on disk, with advice.
   166	    let wanted_sign_in = match wanted(own) {
   167	        Ok(wanted) => wanted,
   168	        Err(why) => {
   169	            advice.push(format!("{app_path} is the starter's {}, but your snapback/*.q {why}", sign_in.label()));
   170	            sign_in.clone()
   171	        }
   172	    };
   173	    let (app_text, mut client_text) = starter::identity_screens(platform, wanted_sign_in.personas(), &wanted_body, wanted_sign_in == SignIn::Password);
   174	    client_text = starter::web_client(client_text, &own.offline);
   175	    let client_changed = client_shape != normalized(&client_text);
   176	    if wanted_sign_in == sign_in && app == app_text && wanted_body == body && !client_changed { return; }
   177	    let mut reasons = Vec::new();
   178	    let mut client_leftover = None;
   179	    if wanted_sign_in != sign_in {
   180	        let (from, to) = (sign_in.label(), wanted_sign_in.label());
   181	        let because = match &wanted_sign_in { SignIn::Personas(_) => "your snapback/*.q declares `use personas` and no `use identity`", SignIn::Password => "your snapback/*.q declares `use identity password`", SignIn::Guest => "your snapback/*.q admits guests" };
   182	        reasons.push(format!("the unmodified starter screen shows {from}, but {because}; adapt writes {to}"));
   183	        // Keep the backend port init wrote.
   184	        if let Some(port) = port_of(&client) { client_text = client_text.replace("127.0.0.1:4400", &format!("127.0.0.1:{port}")); }
   185	        client_leftover = Some(Leftover { path: client_path, why: format!("the unmodified starter client for {from}; adapt writes {to}"), rewrite: Some(client_text.clone()) });
   186	    }
   187	    if wanted_sign_in == sign_in && client_changed {
   188	        if let Some(port) = port_of(&client) { client_text = client_text.replace("127.0.0.1:4400", &format!("127.0.0.1:{port}")); }
   189	        let why = "the unmodified web client needs the device or principal journal selected by your schema's synced tables".to_owned();
   190	        reasons.push(why.clone());
   191	        client_leftover = Some(Leftover { path: client_path, why, rewrite: Some(client_text.clone()) });
   192	    }
   193	    if wanted_body != body {
   194	        reasons.push(match (&wanted_body, &body) {
   195	            (Body::Home(_), Body::Home(Some(op))) => format!("its Home screen reads api.{op}, which your snapback/*.q no longer declares as a query without required arguments; adapt writes the Home screen again{}", match &wanted_body { Body::Home(Some(next)) => format!(", reading api.{next}"), _ => String::new() }),
   196	            (Body::Home(_), _) => "its Todos screen calls the starter's list/add/toggle/remove, which your snapback/*.q does not declare, so it would not typecheck; adapt writes a Home screen that compiles against any schema (the signed-in viewer, your operations from snapback/generated/api.ts, and your first query without required arguments rendered with matchRead) for you to replace".to_owned(),
   197	            (Body::Todos, _) => "your snapback/*.q declares the todo starter's operations again; adapt writes the Todos screen back".to_owned(),
   198	        });
   199	    }
   200	    let why = reasons.join("; ");
   201	    let why = if why.starts_with("the unmodified") { why } else { format!("the unmodified starter screen: {why}") };
   202	    found.push(Leftover { path: app_path, why, rewrite: Some(app_text) });
   203	    found.extend(client_leftover);
   204	}
   205	
   206	/// The `api` path a starter Home's first read calls, if it has one.
   207	fn first_read(app: &str) -> Option<String> {
   208	    let after = app.split_once("function FirstRead()")?.1;
   209	    let op = after.split_once("useQuery(api.")?.1.split_once(',')?.0;
   210	    Some(op.to_owned())
   211	}
   212	
   213	fn read(dir: &Path, path: &str) -> Option<String> {
   214	    let path = dir.join(path);
   215	    path.symlink_metadata().ok().filter(|metadata| metadata.is_file())?;
   216	    std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok()
   217	}
   218	
   219	/// A starter client without what init or the schema varies: the backend port
   220	/// and the persona list.
   221	fn normalized(client: &str) -> String {
   222	    let lines = client.split_inclusive('\n').map(|line| {
   223	        if line.starts_with(PERSONAS) { format!("{PERSONAS}[];\n") } else { line.to_owned() }
   224	    }).collect::<String>();
   225	    match port_of(&lines) { Some(port) => lines.replace(&format!("127.0.0.1:{port}"), "127.0.0.1:4400"), None => lines }
   226	}
   227	
   228	const PERSONAS: &str = "export const personas: readonly string[] = ";
   229	
   230	fn personas_of(client: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   231	    client.lines().find_map(|line| line.strip_prefix(PERSONAS))
   232	        .and_then(|list| serde_json::from_str(list.trim_end().trim_end_matches(';')).ok()).unwrap_or_default()
   233	}
   234	
   235	fn port_of(client: &str) -> Option<&str> {
   236	    let at = client.find("127.0.0.1:")? + "127.0.0.1:".len();
   237	    let digits = client[at..].find(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(client.len() - at);
   238	    (digits > 0).then(|| &client[at..at + digits])
   239	}
   240	
   241	/// One `check` line, or none: the leftovers and the command that fixes them.
   242	pub(crate) fn report(dir: &Path) -> Option<String> {
   243	    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
   244	    if found.is_empty() && advice.is_empty() { return None; }
   245	    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   246	    for leftover in &found { lines.push(format!("  {}: {}", leftover.path, leftover.why)); }
   247	    for line in &advice { lines.push(format!("  {line}")); }
   248	    if !found.is_empty() {
   249	        lines.push("  fix: snapback4 init --adapt (removes or rewrites only these files, which still match the starter; your own files are never touched)".into());
   250	    }
   251	    Some(format!("starter: {} left from `snapback4 init` that {} not fit your schema\n{}",
   252	        if found.is_empty() { "a screen".to_owned() } else { format!("{} file{}", found.len(), if found.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }) },
   253	        if found.len() == 1 || found.is_empty() { "does" } else { "do" }, lines.join("\n")))
   254	}
   255	
   256	/// The same, for `check --json`: `null` when nothing is left over.
   257	pub(crate) fn report_json(dir: &Path) -> serde_json::Value {
   258	    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
   259	    if found.is_empty() && advice.is_empty() { return serde_json::Value::Null; }
   260	    serde_json::json!({
   261	        "files": found.iter().map(|leftover| serde_json::json!({ "path": leftover.path, "why": leftover.why, "adapt": if leftover.rewrite.is_some() { "rewrite" } else { "remove" } })).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
   262	        "advice": advice,
   263	        "fix": if found.is_empty() { serde_json::Value::Null } else { "snapback4 init --adapt".into() },
   264	    })
   265	}
   266	
   267	/// `snapback4 init --adapt [dir]`.
   268	pub(super) fn adapt(dir: &Path) -> i32 {
   269	    match own_schema(dir) {
   270	        Ok(Some(_)) => {}
   271	        Ok(None) => { eprintln!("init --adapt: no snapback/*.q in {}; nothing to adapt to (run `snapback4 init --web` or `--expo` first)", dir.display()); return 1; }
   272	        Err(error) => { eprintln!("init: {error}"); return 2; }
   273	    }
   274	    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
   275	    let mut removed = Vec::new();
   276	    let mut rewritten = Vec::new();
   277	    for leftover in &found {
   278	        let path = dir.join(leftover.path);
   279	        let result = match &leftover.rewrite {
   280	            Some(text) => super::replace_file(&path, text).map(|()| rewritten.push(leftover.path)),
   281	            None => std::fs::remove_file(&path).map(|()| removed.push(leftover.path)),
   282	        };
   283	        if let Err(error) = result { eprintln!("init --adapt: {}: {error}", path.display()); return 2; }
   284	    }
   285	    // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — adapt removes the files it names, never a directory: the next step writes fixtures/<table>.jsonl.
   286	    if found.is_empty() { println!("init --adapt: nothing to adapt — no unmodified starter file conflicts with your snapback/*.q"); }
   287	    if !removed.is_empty() {
   288	        let kept = if removed.iter().any(|path| path.starts_with("fixtures/")) { "; fixtures/ stays for your own rows" } else { "" };
   289	        println!("init --adapt: removed {} (the todo starter's samples{kept}; write your own as fixtures/<table>.jsonl and fixtures/test.cases.jsonl, `snapback4 guide testing`)", removed.join(" and "));
   290	    }
   291	    if !rewritten.is_empty() {
   292	        let why = found.iter().find(|leftover| leftover.rewrite.is_some()).map(|leftover| leftover.why.as_str()).unwrap_or_default();
   293	        println!("init --adapt: rewrote {} — {why}", rewritten.join(" and "));
   294	    }
   295	    if found.iter().any(|leftover| leftover.rewrite.as_deref().is_some_and(|text| text.contains("function Home("))) {
   296	        println!("init --adapt: next: `snapback4 check` writes snapback/generated/api.ts for the Home screen; replace Home with your own screens (`snapback4 guide client`)");
   297	    }
   298	    for line in advice { println!("init --adapt: {line}"); }
   299	    0
   300	}
   301	
   302	#[test]
   303	fn untouched_wave9_screens_still_adapt_and_edited_clients_stay_owned() {
   304	    for platform in [Platform::Web, Platform::Expo] {
   305	        for password in [false, true] {
   306	            let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-w10-adapt-{}-{platform:?}-{password}", std::process::id()));
   307	            std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("snapback")).unwrap(); std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("src")).unwrap();
   308	            let schema = include_str!("starter/schema.q").replace("use identity ", "use identity password ");
   309	            std::fs::write(dir.join("snapback/schema.q"), schema).unwrap();
   310	            let (app, client) = starter::previous_screens(platform, None, &Body::Todos, password);
   311	            let (app_path, client_path) = platform.paths();
   312	            std::fs::write(dir.join(app_path), &app).unwrap(); std::fs::write(dir.join(client_path), &client).unwrap();
   313	            let (changes, _) = leftovers(&dir);
   314	            assert!(changes.iter().any(|change| change.path == app_path), "{platform:?}, password={password}: previous screens recognized");
   315	            std::fs::write(dir.join(client_path), format!("{client}\n// application-owned edit\n")).unwrap();
   316	            let (changes, _) = leftovers(&dir);
   317	            assert!(!changes.iter().any(|change| change.path == app_path || change.path == client_path), "edited pair stays owned");
   318	            std::fs::remove_dir_all(dir).unwrap();
   319	        }
   320	    }
   321	}

    15	/// What init knows about the project before it writes the starter.
    16	pub(super) struct Shape {
    17	    /// The page and app name, from the directory.
    18	    pub title: String,
    19	    pub password: bool,
    20	    /// Personas of a kept schema that declares `use personas` and no
    21	    /// `use identity`: nobody can sign in, so the screen picks a persona.
    22	    pub personas: Option<Vec<String>>,
    23	    /// The screen behind the sign-in: the todo list over the starter's own

    25	    pub body: Body,
